### 9. Why Are Americans So Obsessed With Communism?? (2023-05-31) [link](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2X_GYW_Kteo)
**Metrics:** 743,943 views, 35,671 likes
**Thumbnail description:** A collage of vintage anti-communist propaganda and pulp-magazine imagery fills the frame: a comic cover reading "Is This Tomorrow: America Under Communism" with a burning flag, a movie poster for "I Married a Communist," a tabloid clipping reading "Nameless, Shameless Woman," and a poster for "The Red Menace" billed as "the most talked about drama of our time." Leeja Miller's cutout portrait (glasses, gold jewelry, skeptical sideways glance) is centered. Bold white text on a red paint-stroke banner reading "AMERICA'S OBSESSION" spans the bottom left.
**Opening hook (verbatim, from transcript):**
> On April 30th, 1975, the South Vietnamese capital of Saigon fell to the communist North Vietnamese army. After years of extremely deadly warfare that did nothing to turn the tides of communist takeover of the country, the South Vietnamese army could no longer continue the fight.

**Full transcript (verbatim):**
> On April 30th, 1975, the South Vietnamese capital of Saigon fell to the communist North Vietnamese army. After years of extremely deadly warfare that did nothing to turn the tides of communist takeover of the country, the South Vietnamese army could no longer continue the fight. The United States, after propping up the South Vietnamese and joining the fight against the communist north, had pulled out two years prior, declaring the war a failure and withdrawing aid to the country it had helped destabilize. Now, a few thousand American army and diplomats remained in the country, and with the fall of Saigon, the remaining US citizens, along with the Vietnamese citizens who aided them, and their families, were in imminent danger. In the days leading up to the fall of Saigon, the North Vietnamese army was advancing rapidly, and it quickly became apparent that they would soon take the city. Vietnamese living in Saigon stood in lines outside the American embassy attempting to get visas and flee the oncoming army, knowing that any connection to the US would mean detention or death once the northern army took over. On april 29th, the North Vietnamese began shelling the US Air Base in Saigon, and the mass evacuation began. By this point, The North Vietnamese had blocked all water and airways to and from Vietnam, no planes could land and no ships could come to the rescue. The only way out was via helicopter evacuation to waiting US army ships out in open sea. The US Embassy became the only way to escape Saigon. 10,000 vietnamese pushed against the US Embassy gates hoping to get a spot on the departing helicopters. Pilots flew for 19 hours straight. Helicopters landed in 10 minute intervals across the US embassy grounds, including on the Embassy roof. In less than 24 hours, over 7,000 people were evacuated from Saigon via helicopter, including 5,500 Vietnamese citizens. On April 30th, what remained of the US-backed South Vietnamese government resigned unconditionally, and North Vietnamese tanks drove through the streets of the now occupied Saigon. A military government was instituted, and on July 2, 1976, the country was officially united as the Socialist Republic of Vietnam. What started as US intervention to keep communism from spreading further into Asia ended in a bloody, failed yearslong war that resulted in over 58,000 US military deaths and over 3 million Vietnamese people killed. And for what? Well, to fight the commies of course. The United States’ defeat in Vietnam betrayed an uncomfortable truth that many Americans don’t want to admit to this day: maybe a war against communism can’t be won. Maybe the lines between us and them, between the good guy and the bad guy, once seemingly so clear and incredibly black and white, are much more blurry than our collective imagining of the American consciousness can account for. But how did we get here? Why did the United States take on the fight to root out communism at all costs in the first place? And what has it accomplished? I set out to figure out the answer to this question and what I found frankly can’t be thoroughly summed up in a tidy YouTube video, but I’ll do my best to explain why the United States is so obsessed with Communism. Roll the intro. INTROGrowing up in the United States as a teen in the early 2000s, I remember being taught a pretty whitewashed and sterile version of US history, especially our history in conflicts related to communism. The conflicts we were involved with as a nation were written as almost pre-ordained, this happened because it had to happen, as though history is a series of logical steps as opposed to a messy smattering of events that we have to organize in hindsight. And the United States’ history with communism is certainly messy. But before we get into why the heck we’re so obsessed with it, let’s get some definitions out of the way. What the heck even is communism? Well, to answer that question we also have to answer the question: what is socialism. Because communism is a type of socialism, or an outgrowth of socialism. In the most basic sense, socialism is a social and economic system that involves public, as opposed to private, ownership and control of property and natural resources. Under the socialist view, individuals live in cooperation with one another and everything people produce is in some sense a social product. So everyone who contributes is also entitled to a share in the production of society. Therefore, the society as a whole should own or control property for the benefit of all of its members. This is, of course, an extremely simplified view of socialism, and 2 key divisions have occurred to complicate this doctrine. First, socialists disagree on the extent and kind of property that society should own and control. Should society own everything including the shirt off your back, or is there flexibility for some private ownership–what about small businesses like farms and shops? And second, there is disagreement about the way in which society is to exercise its control over property and resources: via a centralized government? Via a decentralized cooperation of multiple local governments? Something else? These ideas have been around for centuries, but socialism got its formal name around the 1830s, and the idea of socialism as we know it today got its rise during the industrial revolution. Which makes sense. You had a shift from agrarian society to free market capitalism and a conglomeration of power in the hands of large businesses that were then concentrating the wealth in the hands of the few while workers lived in poverty and worked under increasingly dangerous conditions. The Communist Manifesto was published in 1848 by Karl Marx and Friedrich Engels. They criticized early ideals of socialism as being unrealistic utopian dreams. Instead, they advocated for a strong central government to control all aspects of economic production and provide its citizens with their basic needs like food, shelter, medical care, and education. Communism was also sometimes referred to as revolutionary socialism, because Marx and other communist thinkers argued that all of history was, in the end, a history of class struggles. He believed that the working class, or proletariat, would inevitably triumph, through revolutionary struggles, over the wealthy class or the bourgeoisie, gaining control over the means of production and ultimately erasing class distinctions. And this theory was put to the test in Russia during the Russian Revolution of 1917 and the installation of the world’s first communist government in the Soviet Union. In the United States around this time, the socialist party was seeing its heyday. In the years before world war one, the socialist party in the United States claimed 113,000 members, making it one of the largest socialist movements in the world. In the 1912 and 1920 elections, the socialist party gained 900,000 votes, most notably for Eugene V Debs who was running for President, at one point running from prison. I made a whole video about that. In 1919, the party splintered when the communist members split and eventually formed the Communist Party USA. Though their official numbers were smaller, topping out at 66,000 members in 1939, the communist party was able to amass 115,000 votes for senate races in 1940. That all fell apart after World War II. Despite the fact that the United States was briefly allied with Russia to defeat Germany during World War II, immediately after the war, Russia claimed control over much of Eastern Europe instead of reinstating independence to the countries it helped liberate from the Germans, thus forming the USSR. It was in this fallout from world war II that the US obsession with communism took full force. Prior to the second world war, communists and socialists existed in the United States, with some skepticism especially given the human rights abuses committed by the newly formed Soviet Union. But after the second world war the zeitgeist against communism began in full force. This was aided by the founding of the House Un-American Activities Committee, founded in 1938 to root out Nazi sympathizers in the government. The committee became a central force for rooting out communism in all arms of society, including holding highly publicized hearings targeting Hollywood, leading to the creation of a blacklist and ruining the careers and lives of many budding artists in Hollywood for the next decade. In 1947, president Truman put forth what would become known as the Truman Doctrine, pledging that the United States would help any nation resist communism in order to prevent its spread. This policy was also known as the “containment” policy. Like alright, maybe we can’t defeat the Russians, but it has to stop with them. We must contain communism at all costs. That same year, Truman signed Executive Order 9835, which provided the FBI with broad powers to investigate federal employees for potential “security risks” aka communist sympathizers and spies sending information to the Soviets. And, also in 1947, Truman created the Central Intelligence Agency or the CIA and quickly began using the new agency’s covert activities to promote his “containment” theory. Then, in 1949, the Soviet Union exploded its first atomic bomb. And the United States was like ooh noooo we’re supposed to be the only ones capable of ending life on earth with the push of a button. Booo. And then a few months later the Chinese Communist Party officially declared victory in the Chinese civil war that had been raging since 1927. So much for containment. This led to increased fear that secret communists within the United States government had been spying for the Soviets, sending them bomb-making secrets and thwarting communications that could have enabled the US to ward off the communist victory in China. And it was in this national atmosphere, RIPE with fear, that Wisconsin Senator Joseph McCarthy burst onto the scene in February 1950. At what was supposed to be a low profile speech in the small town of Wheeling, West Virginia, McCarthy waved a sheet of paper in the air, proclaiming to the audience “I have here in my hand a list of 205…names that were made known to the Secretary of State as being members of the Communist Party and who nevertheless are still working and shaping [U. S.] policy.” It was later revealed that he didn’t actually have a list, and when pressed for names he waffled, saying he actually had 57 names, and then saying he had 81 names, and then finally promising to disclose the name of a single communist. But even though his evidence was shaky at best, that didn’t matter. He had lit the flame, fanned by fear and paranoia, of what would become known as the Red Scare. Throughout the early 1950s, American leaders regularly told the public to be fearful of the subversive influence of communism in their lives. Communists could be lurking anywhere. On college campuses. In labor unions. Amongst artists and journalists. In your children’s schools. BOO. Senator McCarthy capitalized on this paranoia, launching a series of highly publicized probes into alleged Communist penetration into all arms of the government: the state department, the white house, the treasury, and the Army. At the University of California, 31 professors were dismissed in 1950 for refusing to sign a loyalty oath. The Senate Internal Security Act mandated that all communist organizations had to register with the government, giving the Senate greater power to investigate potential communists for sedition, and revoking or barring citizenship to suspected communists. This anti-communist fervor played out in courtrooms as well. In 1950, a couple named Julius and Ethel Rosenberg, were indicted on charges of giving nuclear secrets to the Russians. Julius had worked briefly at the U. S. Army Signal Corps Laboratory in New Jersey where he had access to classified information. He and his wife Ethel had also been members of the American Communist Party in the 1930s. Based on evidence that was shaky at best, Ethel and Julius were found guilty in 1951 of espionage, under section 2 of the espionage act of 1917, which stated that anyone found guilty of transmitting information relating to national defense to a foreign country could be imprisoned for life or put to death. In June 1953, both Ethel and Julius were executed via the electric chair. Several decades later, evidence came out that definitively showed Julius, but not Ethel, had given information to the Soviet Union. If the Espionage Act of 1917 sounds familiar, that’s because its the same law that Chelsea Manning, Julian Assange, Edward Snowden, and, more recently, former president of the United States Donald J Trump have been accused or charged with violating. The Rosenbergs remain the only people put to death for espionage in peacetime in the United States. The FBI continued to fan the flames of the Red Scare by assisting the production of blatant anti-communist propaganda films and television shows, including The Red Menace in 1949, My Son John in 1951, and I Led Three Lives, a show that ran from 1953-1956. In these films, as well as in other government propaganda and fear mongering, anti-communism was aligned with US pride, overt patriotism, religious conviction, and an unflinching faith in the promise of capitalism and the American dream. These were embedded in the US consciousness, along with anti-communism, as undeniably “American” values. And anything that didn’t align with these values was viewed with suspicion. The paranoia led to a rigid enforcement of conformity. Any behavior considered “deviant” was seen as dangerous. Women, who had taken an important place in the workforce during world war II were told to leave their jobs and go back to the home where they belong. Black people were told to conform to the American social order after fighting and dying for their country. Being gay, already stigmatized, became dangerous as any personal secrets could potentially expose you to blackmail. Many anti-communists, including Senator McCarthy, believed that gay men were morally weak and were therefore particularly predisposed to betraying their country, and lesbians and gay men could be easily blackmailed by Soviet agents because of their deviant sexual behavior. Not only that, but religion, and most importantly Christianity, became a central bulwark for warding off impending communist doom. McCarthy described the anti-communist movement as “an all-out battle between communistic atheism and Christianity.” Religious conservatives pushed for the traditional, nuclear, god-fearing family to protect against the spread of atheistic communism, which was often conflated with totalitarianism after the war. Public displays of religiosity became a prime way in which Americans sought to differentiate themselves from what they considered to be atheist communism. The Pledge of Allegiance was altered to include the words “one nation, under God” in 1954. “In God We Trust” was adopted as the official national motto in 1956. And one of the most popular films of the decade was The Ten Commandments, which retold the story of Exodus as a Cold War parable. Through this paranoia and propaganda, communism became inextricably linked with amorality, and the idea of fighting communism was permanently implanted in the American psyche as inherently moral, christian, patriotic, and the ultimate show of being a true American. And the internal fight to root out communism from all facets of American life, mirrored and was often foreshadowed by external conflicts to further the initial doctrine of containment that President Truman declared at the end of world war II. I can’t give you an exhaustive retelling of the many strings the US pulled internationally in its fight against communism because we ain’t got the time but also because I can guarantee you there’s a lot we simply don’t know. But let me give you a smattering of examples so you can get a feel for it. Because if anti-communism inside America seemed messy, boy howdy was it a catastrophe abroad. KOREA In June, 1950, the North Korean army invaded South Korea, bringing to fruition the United States’ biggest fears and the reason for the containment doctrine. The United Nations and the United States quickly sent troops over to try to push the North Koreans back and keep South Korea from falling to communist rule. Over the course of 3 bloody years, the border between north and south Korea moved dramatically. When the war ended in a truce in 1953, 36,000 American servicemen were dead, and the border between North and South Korea remained largely unchanged from before the war. GUATEMALA In 1950, the same year as the start of the Korean war, Jacobo Arbenz was democratically elected to the presidency in Guatemala. He was not a communist, but he was backed by all the leftist parties in the country. Arbenz was committed to what he termed “modernization” in Guatemala. The main initiative he launched during his presidency was a major agrarian reform bill. As part of that bill, any mass ownership of land where most of the land was not being cultivated would be bought back from the landowners using government bonds and then given to the local peasants to cultivate. Arbenz proposed this bill and the democratically elected parliament passed it. But here’s the pesky thing about land reform. The people with a lot of land really don’t like it. And they often have a lot of money to fight it. Ever heard of United Fruit Company? By 1950, the United Fruit Company was the largest landowner and employer in Guatemala. They also made annual profits of $65 million, which was twice the revenue of the government of Guatemala. And higher ups in the United Fruit Company knew that Americans haaaated the commies, so they started doing some lobbying. The company spent half a million dollars on convincing United States lawmakers and civilians that Arbenz needed to be overthrown. But it wasn’t just powerful lobbying. No no, while the United States was freaked out about communists infiltrating the government, they were perfectly fine with private interests infiltrating every aspect of government operations. Attorney John Foster Dulles had represented the United Fruit Company as their attorney. In 1950, he was the US Secretary of State. His brother, Allen Dulles, was the director of the CIA. Allen also sat on the board of directors of the United Fruit Company. In 1953, Eisenhower authorized the CIA operation to overthrow the government of Jacobo Arbenz. News of this operation was leaked to the Arbenz government, who accused the United States of preparing to overthrow a democratically elected president. The US flatly denied the allegations, and accused Arbenz of falling victim to communist propaganda, which the media and american people ate right up. By 1954, Arbenz was badly in need of weapons. Because the United States had started blocking arms sales to Guatemala in 1951. So he secretly bought weapons from Czechoslovakia. Which of course was then immediately portrayed by the US government as Soviet interference and the establishment of a new communist stronghold in our own backyard!! And that was all the CIA needed to officially launch its coup. By June of 1954, realizing he had lost the support of his army and his people, Arbenz formally resigned the presidency. In the power vacuum left by the coup, a military junta took control of the country, eventually installing Carlos Castillo Armas, a member of the right-wing National Liberation Movement, who created an authoritarian government that was closely allied with the United States. He was the first of numerous authoritarian rulers in Guatemala, whose reversal of Arbenz’ reforms led to the Guatemalan civil war, which lasted from 1960 to 1996. CONGO In 1960, the Republic of Congo declared independence from Belgium rule, and held its first democratic elections. The country elected its first prime minister Patrice Lumumba. Shortly after he assumed office, a Belgian military invasion pushed Lumumba out of power, leading to widespread unrest. And the US, thousands and thousands of miles away, was like yes hi hello we can help. They decided that the unrest provided fertile ground for, what else? Communist invasion obviously. And what’s the only solution, according to the US? Oh yeah, kill Lumumba, the democratically elected prime minister. The CIA encouraged and facilitated Lumumba’s capture and assassination in 1961. This incident helped to instigate the Congo Crisis from 1960 to 1965, during which a military leader named Mobutu Sese Seko consolidated power. In 1965, the CIA supported Mobutu’s coup in the name of preventing the spread of communism. Mobutu became a dictator and ruled the country from 1965 to 1997. CUBA Closer to home, the pro-American military Batista dictatorship in Cuba was overthrown by communist revolutionaries led by Fidel Castro in 1959. The immediate US embargo on Cuba, once a playground for America’s rich and famous, economically cut the island off at its knees. Ironically, the US act of the embargo created, what, yes fertile ground for Soviet influence. Cuba became economically dependent on the Soviet Union. That combined with, what, agrarian reforms that hurt US businesses, plus Cuba’s support of communist movements abroad, all while being located just 90 miles off the coast of Florida, made Cuba a MAJOR communist threat right in America’s backyard. When JFK took office, he was briefed on a plan drafted during the previous administration to use Cuban exiles to invade Cuba, anticipating that the Cuban people would welcome American intervention with open arms, and elements of the Cuban military would join hands with Americans and sing kumbaya and overthrow Castro, installing a non-communist, US-friendly government and living happily ever after. Kennedy approved the operation. On April 17, 1961, 1400 Cuban exiles landed on the Bay of Pigs. The entire force was either killed or captured. The next summer, Russian leader Krushchev had reached a secret agreement with the Cuban government to supply nuclear missiles to the island to protect against another US invasion. American spy planes over Cuba photographed the missile sites as they were under construction. Kennedy responded by placing a naval blockade around Cuba and demanding removal of the missiles. This is commonly known as the Cuban missile crisis, and it was the closest the US got to what many consider mutually assured destruction during the Cold War. Recognizing that the crisis could easily escalate into nuclear war, Khrushchev stepped down, agreeing to remove the missiles in return for a pledge from the Americans that they wouldn’t try to invade Cuba again. DOMINICAN REPUBLICNext door, in the Dominican Republic, long-time dictator Rafael Trujillo was assassinated in 1961. He was a brutal dictator who had, what, the strong support of the United States because he was anti-communist. Great. In fact the United States had occupied the DR from 1916 to 1924 and created the National Police Force, the main vehicle through which Trujillo gained power. Yikes. After Trujillos assassination, the people of the Dominican Republic elected Juan Bosch to the presidency in 1962. But then he was of course overthrown by the military, sending the country into chaos. The United States feared that “another cuba” was happening in the DR, so in 1965 the US sent 22,000 troops to the DR which eventually brought an end to the fighting and installed a conservative US-sympathetic government. VIETNAM Around this same time, in 1963, the United States had sent thousands of US soldiers to Vietnam to help fight against the northern communist government. When the war in Vietnam began, it was assumed that the North Vietnamese would fall quickly. They were seen as less than human, primitive beings who lived in grass huts. President Johnson called Vietnam a “raggedy-ass fourth-rate country.” It was assumed that they didn’t stand a chance against the most technologically advanced society on earth: the United States. By the end of the war a decade later, 58,000 American soldiers were dead, the US retreated in defeat, and the war was the longest armed conflict the United States had ever been involved in, and it had cost them 140 billion dollars. And if you’re like well Leeja that surely must be the end of the story. Such a resounding defeat must have showed the powers in the United States that containment didn’t work, and to maybe be a bit more humble about the United States’ abilities abroad. Well, you’d be wrong my friend. But after Vietnam, the vibe did morph a little bit. Enter, President Reagan. You know him. We talked about him last week. Ever heard of a little thing called the Iran Contra Affair?? Well buckle in kids and let me tell you THIS tale. Throughout the 70s, the US sold a LOT of guns to Iran. Like so many of them. Because it turns out arms sales is an incredibly lucrative business, who knew. Anyway, that was until 1979 when a group of Iranian students seized the US embassy, holding 52 US embassy workers hostage for 444 days until 1981. The Iran hostage crisis firmly cemented anti-American sentiment in Iran and anti-Iranian sentiment in the US and led to an arms embargo against Iran. When Reagan took office in 1981 he was like yeah let’s definitely continue that arms embargo!! And he even went to other countries and was like did you hear about our arms embargo? You also should deeeefinitely not sell arms to Iran. That would be so bad!! At the same time, Reagan was absolutely obsessed with the socialist Sandinista government in Nicaragua. But by the 80s the US had gotten a little smarter?? I guess. At least less willing to openly back violence and coups in other countries. They still did it but like, secretly, you know? And Congress had passed a bill forbidding the use of appropriated funds to back the Contras, a conglomeration of various rebel groups who fought against the Sandinistas in Nicaragua. And Reagan was like nooooo whyyyy. So he found a loophole. Or his subordinates did, it was never conclusively shown how MUCH of this scheme Reagan was privy to, but it happened under his watch. Between 1981 and 1986, Reagan administration officials began secretly facilitating arms sales to Iran. They would then take that money and divert it to the Contras in Nicaragua to support them in their fight against the Sandinistas. It’s a loophole because the funds weren’t TECHNICALLYYY appropriated. The sales of weapons to Iran, in violation of the arms embargo and also, it was revealed, in exchange for hostages from Hezbollah, was a major embarrassment for the Reagan administration because it was literally against the law and also against the stance the administration had publicly taken. US Deputy Attorney General Lawrence Walsh was appointed special counsel to investigate the Iran Contra affair, and 11 Reagan administration officials were eventually convicted. All convictions were later pardoned in the final days of the George H. W. Bush presidency, because he was vice president and pretty shady during the Reagan administration. And the affair, despite being incredibly embarrassing, is but a tiny footnote in the legacy of the Reagan Presidency, which has been built into a heroic myth by Republicans. See my video about Reagan last week for more. And in the years since Vietnam and the Iran Contra affair, the US has continued to covertly stick its nose into affairs. The justification for this meddling, however has changed. With the end of the cold war, symbolized by the fall of the Berlin wall in 1989, the specter of communism has lifted. The fear that country after country would topple to Soviet communist rule was proved meritless. But instead a new fear emerged. Throughout the 1980s, the CIA armed and financed mujahideen rebels fighting the USSR in Afghanistan, because communism, which benefitted, either directly or indirectly depending on who you ask, the newly formed group Al-Qaeda and their leader Osama Bin Laden. On February 26, 1993, a van carrying a homemade bomb entered the lower area of the north tower of the world trade center complex in New York City and detonated. What was meant to send both towers tumbling and kill thousands of people resulted in 6 deaths and the evacuation of 50,000 people in the area. One of the prime conspirators for the bombing was a man named Ramzi Yousef who had spent time at an Al-Qaeda training camp before beginning his plan to bomb the twin towers. Eight and a half years later, when the twin towers did fall, the United States immediately declared a war on terror. Fear of terrorists infiltrating every corner of life gripped the country and led to an extreme increase in violence and hate crimes against middle eastern Americans. George Bush junior announced a global war on terror aimed at thwarting would-be terrorists before they can act. Under this pretext, the United States invaded numerous countries, instigating the longest armed conflict the United States has ever seen, leading to very little “progress” in any war on terror, and ending in what many would consider a resounding failure. Terrorism. Communism. One simply replaced the other in the unrelenting determination that the United States has to be the savior of all and to spread the divine righteousness of American democracy around the globe. Why are Americans obsessed with communism? So, ultimately, what is the answer? Why ARE Americans so obsessed with communism? Even though the cold war has ended, Republicans love to still throw the term around. Trump does it. Marjorie Taylor Greene does it–she calls all Democrats the Communist Party of the United States of America. A recent Pew research study showed that a large majority of Republicans and republican leaning independents held a negative view of socialism, saying that socialism undercuts peoples’ initiative and work ethic, making people too reliant on the government for support, and pointing to socialist failures in other countries as proof that socialism doesn’t work, or simply saying that socialism is not consistent with United States democracy. Anecdotally, if you ever find yourself on the far right side of tiktok, which I don’t recommend, communist fear mongering is alive and well, and according to some the commies have taken over the entire government. As ever, many Republicans use the specter of communism to get American voters to believe that Democrats will bring forth an oppressive future that will mirror the brutal mistakes of communisms past. And terrorism and communism have overlapped in the right-wing American psyche to stand for everything dangerous, oppressive, and unAmerican. During the Republican National Convention, Donald Trump Jr. pledged that a vote for his dad would bring about a world in which the “good guys” persevere over dark forces, saying “Imagine a world where the evils of communism and radical Islamic terrorism are not given a chance to spread–where heroes are celebrated and the good guys win.” And that is just about as good a summation as any to answer the question Why Are Americans Obsessed With Communism. Because in our collective consciousness, we will always be the good guys. We will always be the thwarters of would-be infringements on our freedoms. No no not your sissy left wing freedoms like “choosing what happens to your body” or “reading books” not those freedoms. We mean our AMERICAN freedoms. To be free to amass wealth without government interference. To be free to be Christian. To be free to be a proud, white heterosexual. The United States obsession with communism was created in the 50s when we inextricably linked our national and moral identities as antithetical to communism, as two black and white opposing forces where we are clearly the good guys, endowed with a divine purpose to root out the bad guys at all costs. And that identity has morphed into the radical right we see today, where any whiff of socialist democracy is used to fear monger the masses and where communism and terrorism have become inextricably linked in simpleminded conservatives who believe that being a patriot and a true American is the same thing as having a personality and who see themselves as some strange mixture of GI Joe and Jesus, persecuted and righteous while simultaneously on a mission to single-handedly destroy the dark forces they have been spoon fed to believe are all around them. Because, it turns out that, in the end, being anti-communist is as American as apple pie. If you liked this video, don’t forget to check out my video from last week all about how Reagan ruined everything.

**Structure:** Cold-open dramatized narration of the 1975 Fall of Saigon evacuation, then a definitions section (socialism vs. communism), then a chronological history from the 1848 Communist Manifesto through the 1950s Red Scare/McCarthyism/Rosenberg trial, then a series of labeled Cold War case studies (Korea, Guatemala, Congo, Cuba, Dominican Republic, Vietnam, Iran-Contra, the war on terror), closing with a direct thesis answer tying anti-communism to American national identity and citing a Pew Research study and Donald Trump Jr.'s RNC speech.
**Framing:** Answers-a-question explainer framing (title poses the question, body builds toward an explicit thesis answer at the end) structured as a chronological world-history lecture with repeated labeled country-by-country case studies, using a consistent sarcastic aside voice ('And the United States was like ooh noooo') to undercut the seriousness of the history being recounted; closes by linking the essay back to the channel's own prior video on Reagan.

And the senate is now controlled by a Republican majority. Whether that federal law can trump the state laws across the country protecting abortion is likely a question that will need to be litigated given that the Supreme Court said pretty decisively in Dobbs that the decision should be with the states. But there's also a world where the Supreme Court once again overrules itself in order to allow the federal ban to move forward. And speaking of the supreme court. Clarence Thomas and Samuel Alito will retire during Trump s term. I would if I were them. Oh so I can ride off into the sunset, legacy intact, facing zero consequences for my clear ethical violations, go hang out full-time with my billionaire friends with zero scrutiny, and profit unchecked off that in-tact reputation through book deals and speaking gigs, all while knowing that I will be replaced by equally if not more conservative justices who are much younger than me and can retain the conservative majority in the court long after I'm dead? And I'll be seen as a selfless hero by conservatives everywhere? Yeah sign me up. And with a majority in the Senate, a majority that is no longer filled by any Republicans who are willing to stand up to Trump, Trump will have very little standing in the way of his cabinet or judicial appointees. Not just to the supreme court but to every federal judiciary in the country. That, in turn, will assist with Republican-led efforts to sail above any attempted legal challenges to whatever new batshit crazy law they want to pass. Based on what Trump has promised and what Project 2025 will call for, the manifesto for which will no doubt not only land on Trump s desk on day one but also be distributed to every conservative member of Congress, those crazy laws, either passed by Congress or enacted through Presidential executive order, will include: - Enacting oppressively high tariffs on all goods coming from outside the US, a move economists the world over predict would tank not only the US economy but the entire global economy as well, leading to severe price hikes and job losses - Firing civil servants and changing their jobs to be by presidential appointment, so he can insert loyalists at every agency in the government - Using the impoundment power to block federal funding to agencies - Getting rid of the EPA, the department of education, and severely limiting the capacities of key agencies that protect consumers and regulate the safety of everything from pharmaceuticals to deli meats. - Lina Khan, head of the FTC, has been hard at work suing the pants off mega corporations for their unconscionable monopolistic practices. She will be out and any lawsuit underway will be terminated. Corporations, whose billionaire owners were hard at work this election season throwing money at Trump in the hopes of this exact outcome, will be able to grow unchecked, knowing they will face little to no consequences for their actions - Carrying out the largest deportation in history, including re-instating the Muslim ban, remain in Mexico, and other policies that saw children locked in cages at the border. This could include rescinding DACA, refugee protections, and many other policies that have allowed people to immigrate legally to this country, and the deportation of those immigrants who came in through those legal channels. If successful, the deportation plan will cost billions of dollars and will likely scoop up lawful residents and US citizens in their dragnets. - Mass tax cuts for the rich, which will further increase the federal deficit, all but ensuring the end to social security benefits - Deep cuts to medicare, medicaid, and all other social safety nets that millions of people rely on throughout the country - Withdrawal from the Paris climate agreement, removal of tax incentives for green initiatives, and a deep expansion of our reliance on fossil fuels - Withdrawal from NATO and a deepening divide between the US and our historic allies - And more. And all of this will go largely unchecked by a judiciary that will be dominated by conservative Trump appointees from top to bottom. This is all stuff we've been talking about on this show for years at this point. And I don't mean to freak you out by presenting them all to you again, but I'm someone who tends to want to face the facts and find solutions. Today, I don't really have solutions for you but I do have facts. This is the part where I'm supposed to give you a pep talk and say hey listen we live to fight another day. The arc of the universe bends towards justice. We can't let them win. I'm not feeling that today. I've been reconsidering my decision not to move to Europe, which I made a whole episode about. I've been wondering if there's any point to continuing this show because clearly I'm screaming into a void. I'm going to continue because I do care, and I have to pay my bills. But I'm just being honest about what's been going through my head in the panic I've been feeling since about 10 o clock last night when I realized it wasn't going our way. Saying hey let's keep fighting is not in my heart right now, and it's fine if it's not in yours either. This IS America. The fight is very hard when fully over half the electorate lives a very different life from the other half. We don't interact. We don't live in the same areas. We don't consume the same media. We possess values that are fundamentally different. We have worldviews that are diametrically opposed. I've made optimistic episodes about how important it is to talk to people you don't agree with but today at least I'm feeling pretty pessimistic about any of that mattering. But I am a solutions oriented person, if nothing else, so despite feeling a combination of numbness and despair, and not being in a place to give y all a pep talk right now, I am interested in talking solutions. The reality is we cannot rely on the federal government to do anything for us. We cannot rely on the federal courts to check them either. At the very least we can rely at least in part on, hopefully, SOME adherence to the central Republican tenet of states rights. We have to focus locally. I made a whole episode on HOW to get involved in local politics, PLEASE check that out, it is more important than ever. Who s your governor? Your mayor? Who are your state representatives? Who represents you on city council? Follow their social media, put their numbers on speed dial, save their email addresses. Find a local advocacy organization doing work you care about. They typically track local legislation and will have information so you can learn what's happening locally and reach out to your representatives to talk about the issues. Push for non-winner take all elections, like ranked choice voting, to help grow the voices of third parties. Your voice is SO much louder at the local level than it is on the national level. Especially if you are part of a group of like-minded people who you can represent when you go visit your legislator, and say me and this entire group feel this way about this piece of legislation, that has a powerful amount of sway. In places like here in Minnesota, we pushed further right this election, but Minneapolis is still overwhelmingly blue, and the momentum to the right isn't too big to overcome. If you live in a red state, this will still be an uphill battle. If I'm being completely honest with you, if I lived in a red state I would leave. I know that's not possible for many people for so many reasons but I would do my fuckin darndest to figure out a way to make it work. But that's just because today, at this moment in time, holy fuck I'm so exhausted. I would tap the fuck out if faced with those odds. Maybe at the very least move to a blue part of your red state. But, despite those odds, there is progress that can be made at the local level even in the reddest of states. Whether that's finding the blue dot city or making your voice as loud as possible despite the sea of red, there is power in keeping it local. Because despite my exhaustion and numbness right now, there is a spark inside me that makes me feel like I want to be ungovernable. I don't mean like an anarchist burning down buildings. I mean, like, Sinead o Connor ungovernable. Shave your head. Tear up pictures of the pope. Wear weird shit. Be unapologetically loud at city council meetings, I don't know. For now, though, I'm gonna end this video a little short, and I'm gonna go lay on the couch for a while. And that's ok, too. Take care of yourself and the people closest to you. That s a radical act in itself. If you want ad free and uncensored versions of these videos, join me over on Patreon at patreon dot com slash leejamiller. Thank you to my multi-platinum patrons, Rex, Tee, L Etranger (Lukus), Joshua Cole, Thomas Johnson, Anthony Jiles, Tay, and Bret Piontek. Thanks so much for your support! Please check out my episode about how to get involved in local politics.

**Structure:** Cold-open same-day election-results rundown (electoral college, popular vote, Senate flip), a sponsor read demonstrating a news-bias tool on the election headlines themselves, then a personal, less-scripted reaction section processing the results emotionally, a data section citing NBC exit polling on economic sentiment, then a bulleted forecast list of specific Trump/Project 2025 policy moves expected next, closing with a locally-focused solutions section (state/city government, ranked-choice voting) and an admission she does not have an uplifting pep talk to offer.
**Framing:** Same-day news-reaction diary framing, explicitly less polished/scripted than the channel's usual essay format ('I couldn't be bothered to put makeup on'), mixing hard exit-poll data with raw first-person emotional processing; ends on a deliberately muted, non-triumphant call to local political action rather than the channel's more common confident indictment closer.

**Structure:** Cold open stitching together sound-bite-style impressions of a Trump rally (Puerto Rico 'floating island of garbage' joke, birther-style birthplace riffing, 'one more week' chant) before the narrator identifies the clip as the actual Madison Square Garden rally, then a sponsor read (Lumen, a metabolism tracker), then a rundown of the specific rhetoric at that rally (the Puerto Rico joke, speaker lineup) used as evidence for the video's central claim, closing with the standard Patreon thank-you and cross-promo to a Christian-nationalism video.
**Framing:** News-hook framing keyed to a single real event (the MSG rally) rather than a slow-build argument; opens by replaying the rally's own ugliest lines before the narrator steps in to name what they add up to, so the audience hears the evidence before the verdict.

Understanding how we got here requires us to take a dive, as usual, into history, to discover both who these people are that continue to support Trump, but also who Trump is; how the myth of him took hold and allowed him to grow to power in the first place. Donald Trump was born on June 14, 1946, he is, say it with me, a GEMINI. Classic. His father Fred Trump was a real estate developer and after Donny Trump somehow managed to graduate from college with a degree in economics despite having seemingly never read a book once ever in his entire life, he then joined daddy managing apartment complexes in Queens and Brooklyn in the 1970s. By 1973 they were charged with violating the Fair Housing Act by actively discriminating against black tenants. That won’t stop them though! Next they bought the Commodore Hotel, revamped it and reopened it as the Grand Hyatt Hotel, kicking off Trump’s career as a Manhattan developer. By 1987 Trump solidified his reputation as a businessman, as the ken doll whose job is business[a], when he “wrote” his first book The Art of the Deal which became a bestseller. He was so so good at doing deals and being business that by 1990 he was nearly $1 billion dollars in personal debt, though he managed to slither his way out of that one somehow. Then he did more business, filing for bankruptcy for the Trump Taj Mahal in 1991 and the Trump PLaza and Castle Casinos in 1992. And while he was businessing around, he was also thinking hmm business is cool but what is one step above business man? Oh yeah, PRESIDENT. And in 1999 he went on Larry King to say that he’s planning on challenging Pat Buchanan for the Reform Party nomination for president. When that didn’t work in 2000 he said NEVER MIND!! And then decided you know what I am such business that I should probably star in a show about business man, and The Apprentice was born. By this point Trump had solidified himself in popular culture as a Manhattan millionaire playboy, with frequent cameos on movies and TV, including in Home Alone 2, Sex and the City, The Fresh Prince, The Nanny, The Little Rascals, and Zoolander. To such an extent that in 2000 the Simpsons, in one of its many prescient moments, showed a future where Trump was president. Writer Dan Greaney said in 2016 “What we needed was for Lisa to have problems beyond her fixing, that everything went as bad as it possibly could be, and that’s why we had Trump be president before her. That just seemed like the logical last stop before hitting bottom. It was consistent with the vision of America going insane.” So Trump as president, as outlandish as it seemed to most of us when he announced his run for 2016, wasn’t completely out of thin air. And the political atmosphere was shockingly ripe for his picking. The Washington Post has aptly tied Trump’s rise to power to the 2008 financial crisis. When Obama was elected, one of the first things he did was pass a massive stimulus bill to address the recession, adding to the national debt and bailing out big businesses while working class people suffered and lost their jobs and their homes. Democrats subsequently experienced major losses in the 2010 midterm elections as a result of this backlash. During this time, the US also saw the growth of the Tea Party movement, the first murmurs of what was to come with MAGA and the alt-right, and a group that picked up speed as a backlash to Obama’s election and progressive policies grew. It was a political movement that, thanks to the then only decade old internet, was able to consolidate and proliferate its ideas on a scale never seen before, so that once isolated groups of people with fringe beliefs were able to communicate and collaborate for the first time. This is the group that housed the birthers who were convinced that Obama wasn’t a citizen, and used fear tactics to foment conspiracy theories and activate the conservative base to vote. But they weren’t strong enough to take the white house. After losing again in 2012, the Republicans took a step back to look at what they were doing wrong. Because, as we’ve discussed in previous videos, politics isn’t about what’s best for America or what your constituents actually want, it’s about figuring out which one or two wedge issues their base will get riled up enough about to show up to the polls for. A Republican Growth & Opportunity Project report from 2012 showed that Republicans needed to champion comprehensive immigration reform. Add to that the loss of the gay marriage fight in 2015 and the ongoing fervor over abortion, and you had yourself a slew of wedge issues that got conservatives riled up. And Trump said exactly what it took to do just that. As early as 2011, Trump was on Good Morning America questioning whether Obama was born in the US. In June 2015 when he announced he was running for president, he said he would implement policies to boost the economy and get tough on immigration, a few months later calling for a “complete shutdown of Muslims entering the United States.” And his ability, despite being an out of touch millionaire, to speak to his base of working class supporters and make them feel heard and seen really resonated. He was saying the racist shit they think is the “truth,” tying immigration to the economic losses they had been experiencing since the great recession, when they watched big businesses receive handouts from the government, and lost their jobs but saw black and brown folks and immigrants sometimes succeeding when they thought they were here first and deserve to win. Trump was up there giving them easy answers to the problems they were facing. And honestly, this is just sales 101, people. When you’re trying to sell someone something they gotta know, like, and trust you. You have to address their objections and tell them why you’re the solution. And you need to speak in a language they understand, so that they feel like they’re being seen and heard, and that you will deliver the answer to the problem they need solved. Whether you’re selling microwaves, developing real estate, or running for president, it’s all in the art of the deal. And despite his many failures in business, Trump managed to figure it out this time. And if you’re able to manipulate people into knowing, liking, and trusting you enough, no matter where you go and what shit you peddle they’ll be there to hand you all their money. And no one in politics has managed to develop such a strong customer base as Trump. They’re ready to buy whatever he’s selling. But who actually are the people who support Trump? Well due in part to the fact that many of his most fervent supporters are anti-establishment conspiracy theorists who don’t trust the media or politicians, getting them to respond to polls about politicians by the media is pretty impossible. But based on the population that does actually respond to polls, we know that those who voted for Trump in 2020 were 85% white, 58% were non-college educated whites, and 15% voters of color including 8% latinos. Polls showed that the less educated you were, the more likely you were to vote for Trump. And the older you were the more likely you were to vote for Trump. And the more rural your town the more likely you were to vote for Trump. And the more money you made the more likely you were to vote for Trump. None of this is surprising given what we know about what divides us as a country. But it doesn’t fully explain the level of fervent loyalty. Recent polling by NPR, PBS NewsHour and Marist found that 64% of Republicans said they wanted Trump to be president again, even if he was found guilty of a crime. Trump has managed to condition his supporters to believe that any legal challenge against him is invalid and a product of some conspiracy or the deep state trying to persecute truth tellers like him (and, by extension, his supporters). Berkeley scholars have found that Trump won with an unexpected coalition of white working class and middle class voters who were motivated by resentment. “The culture and economy gave them no recognition and no respect for their work. Their industries were changing, their jobs were shifting overseas or lost to automation. They perceive that black, latine, and asian people, and immigrants are advancing at their expense.” How do his supporters, who are more likely to be Christian and to value law and order, rectify their beliefs with their support of a proven adulterer, compulsive liar, millionaire tax cheat, and now multiple-times charged criminal defendant? [show clip of woman telling Jordan Klepper “I don’t care”] The answer lies less in politics or logic and more in populism, mind control, and cults. According to scholar of 20th century authoritarian politics Ruth Ben-Ghiat, Trump “differed from past heads of state of either party in having zero interest in public welfare or consensus politics. His goals were autocratic: amassing power, domesticating the GOP, and having his financial and other personal interests prevail over national ones in shaping domestic and foreign policy.” His presidency was unprecedented in the United States, but we’ve seen this before elsewhere in history. Juan Peron ruled over Argentina after a coup in 1943 instated him as the head of a military dictatorship. However, by 1946 fascism was really not cool, so fascist rulers were like rats we need a rebrand. And they saw democratic elections as a means of legitimizing their rule. So in 1946 he ran on a populist ticket, winning the presidential election in Argentina and becoming the first populist leader in history to come to power. Peron’s populism shared some pretty foggy gray areas with fascism. For example, he did not like liberal politics, denounced “ruling elites,” undercut the legitimacy of independent journalism, espoused a deep dislike of pluralism and political tolerance, and cultivated a cult of personality that allowed him to be democratically elected despite his decidedly undemocratic, authoritarian principles. (Sound familiar?) Other populist leaders came to power, blurring the lines of democracy and pushing their countries closer to autocracy and fascism. Leaders like Bolsonaro in Brazil, Benjamin Netanyahu in Israel, and Keiko Fujimori in Peru promote their own version of history, fomenting a distrust in government and in their country’s collective understanding of their own history to create an alternate reality where they can rule legitimately while simultaneously promoting undemocratic, authoritarian objectives. Professor Yascha Mounk at Johns Hopkins University defines a fundamental throughline of populist thinking that includes “a way of thinking about the world that holds that the only reason we have problems is because of a political leader that is corrupt and self-serving, cares more about various outsiders than they do about real people” and to fix these problems, a single individual who truly represents the people needs to be elected so that they can do away with the corruption, seize back power from entrenched elites, and get rid of anyone who stands in the way of the “will of the people.” This involves 2 key elements: anti-elitism, meaning that there’s something wrong with the people in charge and anti-pluralism, meaning that one leader alone represents the will of the people, and if you disagree with that person, you are illegitimate and you are a traitor to the people and must be taken down. And these ideas pose a fundamental threat to liberal democratic ideals of collective self-determination, meaning rule by the people, as well as individual rights and freedoms, including independent institutions like the media and separate checks and balances on presidential power. Two ways to further the populist cause and curb these perceived threats to the will of the populist leader is to foment distrust in the media and undermine the independence of the judiciary. To claim as the leader that you are the only one who can speak on behalf of the people. To be willing to push the confines of what’s legal to the max in order to maintain power. And to be very adept at providing very simple answers to peoples’ problems. The reason you don’t have a job is because of immigrants. The reason you can’t get ahead is because of affirmative action. The reason why drug use in your community is growing is because of immigrants. Etc. It’s an us vs. them mentality that posits that if you are not behind this leader, the one that speaks for the will of the people, then you are against us and we must take you out. It’s truly wild to read about scholars dissecting the populism play book because it makes me really question my theory that Trump is illiterate because he follows it to a T in a way that seems too scholarly and accurate to be by happenstance. As professor Vittorio Hosle who studies philosophy and political science put it, “It is an intrinsic danger in modern democracies that for many reasons—the erosion of the working class, the creation of a lot of people who feel declassed and not taken seriously in the globalized world—it is likely that pressure towards authoritarianism will increase everywhere.” Hosle points to 2016 as the year when this turn towards global authoritarianism picked up momentum. It was the year of the Brexit referendum, an attempted coup in Turkey, the election in the Philippines of a president who openly bragged that he would kill criminals and then pardon himself, and the election of Donald Trump, who openly bragged he could shoot someone on 5th Avenue in broad daylight and get away with it. The United States was recently added to the International Institute for Democracy and Electoral Assistance list of “backsliding democracies.” And the most obvious outcome of this rise in populism, where the cult of one leader trumps everything else, including logic, reason, and fundamental democratic ideals, is highly polarized countries. And this polarization can have devastating consequences for democracy, because it creates a country and an electorate obsessed with an “us vs. them” mentality, with half of the population willing to follow a man who has claimed he’s the “chosen one.” As Professor Jennifer Chatman puts it “He’s said he’s super-smart, a genius. … He has established his image as the leader who is cleaning up Washington and the savior of the common person so convincingly that none of his supporters are looking beyond that to see that, in fact, many of the things he’s doing are exactly the opposite.” But that doesn’t matter to a Trump supporter, and I think the reason for this is in part because he’s established himself as a populist leader who represents the will of “true Americans” but it’s more than that. As one recent article out of Berkeley said, Trump followers exhibit “a loyalty to Trump that is so intense, and so unshakeable, that it exerts a cult-like gravity.” In addition to the fact that Trump has openly courted cults like QAnon to such an extent that 56% of Republicans believe that the absolutely bizarre conspiracies being touted by QAnon are somewhat or entirely true, Trump himself has taken a cue from the behavior of cults and cult leaders in how he has implanted himself in the psyche of half the electorate. Steven Hassan is a mind control expert, mental health professional, and spent 2 and a half years in the Moonie cult before being deprogrammed. He wrote a book called The Cult of Donald Trump and drew some stark lines between cults and the following Trump has amassed. For one, a cult usually involves a venerated leader, someone who is considered king or god-like. Everyone is falling over themselves to praise the leader and be in the leader’s good graces. They use loaded language that is evocative and creates a strong image in peoples’ minds while also being incredibly vague and lacking a clear definition, goal, or outcome that is based in reality. For example “lock her up” “fake news” “witch hunt” “build a wall” the “deep state”, “Make America Great Again.” Cults thrive where its members are sleep deprived and bombarded with information overload, especially in silos where they are not receiving any information other than that which is being created and promoted by the cult. But Hassan is not the only person to draw links between Trump’s followers and cult-like behavior. Robert Reich for Now This has also drawn the comparison, noting Trump’s intolerance for criticism and dissent, and anyone who does take issue with Trump are called liars, traitors, and phonies. Trump’s tactics are also culty because he claims and his supporters genuinely believe that Trump alone knows the truth, using fear as a key ingredient to foment blind loyalty and consolidate his base. This all while actively and publicly doing things against the interests of his supporters and in complete opposition to his promises, like cutting medicare and medicaid, lining billionaire’s pockets, filling his cabinets with entrenched corporate executives and lobbyists who are arguably some of the deadliest crocodiles in the swamp he’s claiming to drain. One tool experts use to diagnose the severity of cult-like behavior is the BITE model, which is an acronym that stands for Behavior, Information, Thoughts, and Emotional control. If we look at the factors in each, a number of them stand out. Under the B for Behavior, one important tactic is “Major time spent with group indoctrination and rituals and/or self-indoctrination including the internet”. Under I for information, notable tactics include Use of deception, minimizing or discouraging access to non-cult sources of information, and Compartmentalization of information meaning categorizing ideas and doctrines as either insider or outsider ways of thinking, and extensive use of cult-generated information and propaganda. Under T for Thoughts, notable tactics include requiring cult members to internalize the group’s doctrine as the one and only truth, using loaded language and cliches to constrict knowledge, stop critical thoughts and reduce complexities into platitudes and buzz words. Manipulating memories and creating false memories (make a America great again? What does that mean? When are we talking? Great for whom? It doesn’t make sense). Also rejecting rational analysis, critical thinking, or constructive criticism, Forbidding critical questions about the cult leader, doctrine, or policy, and labeling alternative belief systems as illegitimate, evil, or not useful. And then finally E for emotional control includes tactics such as instilling fear in the cult members, including irrational fears about leaving the group or questioning the leader’s authority. This threat of shunning is an incredibly powerful tool as cult members have often distanced themselves from everyone in their life “before” the cult, so they have no one and have placed the entirety of their lives and personalities in this cult. It explains why, for example, sons were willing to turn their own fathers in for participating in the January 6th insurrection. They were too far gone, they had pushed away their own families and there was a distinct difference between their life, actions, and beliefs before Trump and after Trump. All of these tactics have been used to certain degrees throughout Trump’s campaigns and presidency and they continue to unite a shocking number of people. One thing that struck me in all of these tactics is the importance of information, and controlling the narrative via the information that cult members consume. This is a huge part of Trump’s tactics, made all the easier because of the silo-ing of people by political party in this country. If he wants to control the narrative for his base, all he has to do is post on Truth Social or call Fox News and he’s reached pretty much all of them. Internet conspiracy theories do the rest. He has masterfully used the media in ways that would make any cult leader proud. Trump has employed all of these tactics and then some, leading his followers to believe that every bad thing that happened while Trump was president was not because of his policies but in spite of them. That he is an innocent man forever being framed. He’s capitalized on the us vs. them mentality, causing his base to catastrophize the threats of the left into existential dangers that require a literal uprising to overcome. [insert some tiktok reaction videos] And at this point, 8 years into Trump as candidate and President, his supporters are so entrenched that even if on the off chance someone were to get through to them, to make them care about logic and show them the illogical nature of their beliefs, they’re too far gone. As a recent article in the Atlantic put it, “[F]or Trump supporters to call him out would be to call out themselves, and that’s too painful for too many people. The greater the ethical compromises we make, the fiercer our justifications become—and the angrier and more frustrated we get at those who won’t go along for the ride.” Essentially, half of America has signed a deal with the devil in aligning itself with Trump, with the hate and resentment he espouses and proliferates, and now must double down because it’s too painful to admit they were wrong. And this is the case for Trump’s supporters both in the electorate and in the government. His ideology has pushed the GOP so far to the right that anyone who’s moderate or you know just generally somewhat sane within the GOP has been alienated or forced to concede to the far right faction. This is evidenced by the general lack of condemnation of Trump despite his behavior and his 3 almost 4 criminal indictments. The cult of Donald Trump is so strong that anyone who wants a political future within the GOP is afraid to criticize him. Even DeSantis, the man running against him for president, knows that to be overly critical of him would be a death sentence. Polls show DeSantis doesn’t really have a chance of getting the nomination over Trump at this point but his numbers likely would be lower if he was actively railing against Trump as is typical of most political rivalries. And in the scenario where we have a 2020 redo, a Trump vs. Biden race in 2024, polls are currently showing Trump and Biden in a dead heat for the White House. But who knows what that’ll look like in 15 months when we’re actually at the voting booths. 15 more months of legal actions against Trump. 15 more months of him using those indictments and prosecutions to his advantage, to further his narrative that he and, by extension, his followers are being persecuted by the deep state and that he and he alone is the savior, much like Jesus, who must rise again to fight for his people. With each indictment he gains in the polls and increases his budget with a pouring in of donations. And he’s continuing to manipulate the media in all his usual ways, posting on Truth Social that “This is nothing more than the latest corrupt chapter in the continued pathetic attempt by the Biden Crime Family and their weaponized Department of Justice to interfere with the 2024 Presidential Election, in which President Trump is the undisputed frontrunner, and leading by substantial margins.” The statement went on to compare the legal actions against Trump (and, thereby, against his supporters) with Nazi Germany. Which is ironic given what we know about how closely populism and fascism like to dance, but yeah sure. And he’s repeating his playbook from 2020 by alleging that he is the “undisputed frontrunner, and leading by substantial margins.” That is factually inaccurate, but it doesn’t matter. Not to his supporters at least. And this is what he did, over and over again, before the 2020 election, and it got his followers amped up and scared to such a degree that they were willing to attempt to overthrow the government for it. Keep an eye out for him trying to delegitimize voting methods like mail-in ballots and drop boxes, that’s the next step from his 2020 playbook. And who knows what will happen in January 2025 if he once again loses to Biden. The Washington Post recently gave me some heartening news in that the three tactics Trump relied on to try to overturn the 2020 election are no longer available to him. For one thing, in 2020 he tried to replace electors in key states via Republican-controlled state legislatures, based on the absolutely bat-shit “independent state legislatures doctrine” that said that the Constitution gave state legislatures more power than the federal government to determine how elections were managed. The supreme court did away with that doctrine this year in their Moore v. Harper decision. Another tactic he used was attempting to appeal to Republicans in key states to quietly put a thumb on the scales. A number of key states flipped Democratic in the midterms, so there are fewer Republicans for him to appeal to, and he’s literally about to be indicted for doing that, so people will be on high alert for repeat behavior. And then the final tactic was the attempt on January 6th to stop or reverse Biden’s final certification in Congress. This time, however, Trump’s VP Mike Pence won’t be the one overseeing the certification. It’ll be Biden’s VP, Kamala Harris, which closes out that avenue for Trump to attempt to overturn the election results. There’s also the helpful fact that Trump is not the President, so there is no transfer of power to be had in the event that Biden wins again. Keeping the status quo is much easier than peacefully transferring power. And yet even so, after the 2021 insurrection, we managed to transfer power. So I’m grasping onto those whisps of hope for dear life, despite the fact that some journalists are worried that, without these quasi-”legal” avenues for Trump to use to try to overturn the 2024 election, he will opt instead for a more overtly revolutionary approach. As Professor Ruth Ben-Ghiat, who studies 20th century populism and fascist leaders, said “It takes prosecution and conviction to deflate their personality cults.” Which begs the question, could Trump still be elected president if he’s a convicted criminal? I made a whole video about this, but while it’s unprecedented for someone to become president while actively incarcerated, it doesn’t appear to technically be unconstitutional. There are only 3 requirements to be president. You must be born in the US, over 35, and a resident of the US for at least 14 years. Candidates with criminal convictions have run in the past, and socialist Eugene V. Debs, who you’ll remember helped organized the largest strike in history in the late 1800s, ran for president while in federal prison in Atlanta as the nominee of the socialist party in 1920. He was convicted for violating the espionage act, which Trump is also charged with. He won 3% of the vote, which is pretty good for a third party candidate. And given how much Trump has gained in the polls since his indictments, there is a very possible world in which Trump runs for president from prison, and a less probable but still possible world in which Trump is elected president while serving time in a federal penitentiary. That being said, there is the one pesky hangup in the 14th Amendment, which prevents anyone from running for office who has taken an oath of office and thereafter engaged in “insurrection or rebellion” against the country. Which makes Jack Smith’s prosecution all the more pivotal truly for American history. Because if he is found guilty of instigating that January 6th insurrection, it could bar him from running for office again. Absent that conviction, scholars have been weighing in on whether Trump could pardon himself. The general answer seems to be that he could try, but it would be challenged in court and likely overturned. Plus, a Presidential pardon only applies to federal cases. The criminal case against him involving the Stormy Daniel hush money is a state case. And in the event that he serves jail time for that case, which is unlikely but possible, Congress could invoke the 25th Amendment to impeach him for being unable to perform his duties. Either way, in the event that Trump wins the election in 2024, we would be in for a lengthy series of court battles that could upend our stability and make us seem even weaker on a global scale than we already are thanks to his first presidency. So, friends, it doesn’t look good. Please vote. If you liked this video, you might also like my recent video about how the religious right ruined everything. Thank you to my Patreon supporters, including my newest patrons, and an extra special shout out to my multi-platinum patron Brett Piontek. If you’re interested in behind the scenes content, access to my research and show notes, content about my dog, and all sorts of other stuff, consider joining me over on Patreon today.

**Structure:** Opens with a rapid rundown of Trump's stacking 2023 criminal indictments (January 6th/fraud, hush money, classified documents, a fourth expected in Georgia), pivots to the video's real question, why each indictment correlates with rising polling and fundraising rather than decline, then a sponsor read (Warby Parker), then a structured argument mapping cult-recruitment and cult-loyalty literature onto MAGA behavior, closing with a direct voting appeal and the standard Patreon/cross-promo sign-off.
**Framing:** Explainer-with-a-thesis framing: the news recap is scaffolding for an extended analogy (political movement as literal cult), signaled early by the thumbnail's cult-rally imagery and cashed out through the body of the video rather than left as a headline-only tease.

### 13. Why Are Conservatives So Obsessed With Trans Kids? (2023-06-14) [link](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=X6qUxa30SFA)
**Metrics:** 599,185 views, 33,146 likes
**Thumbnail description:** Leeja Miller's cutout portrait (glasses, gold hoop earrings, white top) centered against a backdrop of overlapping real news headline clippings in blue, mauve, and grey stripes ('Majority of Americans oppose anti-trans bills...', 'DeSantis signs flurry of orders including ban on gender-affirming care for minors', 'Missouri governor signs bill...'), with a large white 'anti-trans' text clipping layered on top.
**Opening hook (verbatim, from transcript):**
> ﻿Nearly 500 anti-trans bills have been introduced in states across the country this year alone. They target everything from books and drag shows to gender affirming care, access to bathrooms, and even just speaking about the existence of trans people in schools. The issue of trans rights, which affects less than 1% of the US population, has become the central rallying cry for republicans.

Through widespread voter registration, fundraising, and utilizing the media to inform voters about “the cause” and their version of “the truth” the Moral Majority was able to play a large role in getting Ronald Reagan elected president in 1980, solidifying the religious right’s outsized role in politics and presidential elections that persists today. Since then, the religious right has fixated on “social issues” like abortion, religious-based education, LGBTQ rights, and parent’s rights. Because, again, this has never been about protecting children or Christian morals, it’s always been about power–getting elected and staying elected. Especially if the issue was framed as a question of whether to allow “men and boys to compete against women and girls.” It’s common knowledge in political polling and analysis that the form of a question matters, and that in order to get unbiased results you need to frame the question in a way that doesn’t lead the pollee to a certain outcome or conclusion. But the opposite can also be weaponized by politicians. Instead of asking “should trans girls compete on girls sports teams?” which is almost obvious in its outcome–yes trans girls–girls sports team, makes sense. In March 2020, Idaho became the first state to bar transgender girls from participating in girls and women’s sports, which was called the “Fairness in Women’s Sports Act.” And one year later, Arkansas became the first state to enact legislation making it illegal for minors to receive transition medication or surgery. This legislation was based on model language first used in South Dakota that didn’t initially pass there but created the framework that was then used nationwide to curb trans rights. The model language was created by lawmakers in cahoots with religious-right affiliated groups including the Child & Parental Rights Campaign, the Alliance Defending Freedom, the Phyllis Schlafly-founded Eagle Forum, and a fringe conservative doctors group that calls itself the American College of Pediatricians. Emails obtained by Mother Jones show members of those groups pushing lawmakers to make the laws more restrictive, and to ensure the language was ironclad.
